**Giorgia Meloni** (Italian: [\[ˈdʒordʒa meˈloːni\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/Italian); born 15 January 1977) is an Italian politician who has served as [Prime Minister of Italy](273741.md) since October 2022. She is the first and only woman to hold the office and the [head of the longest-serving government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Italian_governments) in the [history of the Italian Republic](1161302.md).[^cite_note-1][^cite_note-2] A member of the [Chamber of Deputies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chamber_of_Deputies_\(Italy\)) since 2006, she has been president of the [right-wing](56522.md) to [far-right](221220.md) party [Brothers of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brothers_of_Italy) (FdI) since 2014, and was president of the [European Conservatives and Reformists Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Conservatives_and_Reformists_Party) from 2020 to 2025.

In 1992, Meloni joined the [Youth Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Youth_Front_\(Italy\)), the youth wing of the [Italian Social Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Social_Movement) (MSI), a [neo-fascist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neo-fascist) political party founded in 1946 by followers of [Italian fascism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_fascism). She later became the national leader of [Student Action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Student_Action_\(Italy\)), the student movement of the [National Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Alliance_\(Italy\)) (AN), a [post-fascist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post-fascism_in_Italy) party that became the MSI's legal successor in 1995 and moved towards [national conservatism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_conservatism). She was a councillor of the [province of Rome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Province_of_Rome) from 1998 to 2002, after which she became the president of [Youth Action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Youth_Action_\(Italy\)), the youth wing of AN. In 2008, she was appointed [Minister for Youth Policies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_for_Youth_Policies) in the [fourth Berlusconi government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fourth_Berlusconi_government), a role which she held until 2011.&#x20;

In 2012, she co-founded FdI, a legal successor to AN, and became its president in 2014. She unsuccessfully ran in the [2014 European Parliament election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_European_Parliament_election) and the [2016 Rome municipal election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Rome_municipal_election). After the [2018 general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Italian_general_election), she led FdI in opposition during the entire [18th legislature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/18th_Italian_legislature). FdI grew its popularity in opinion polls, particularly during the management of the [COVID-19 pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Italy) by the [Draghi Cabinet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Draghi_Cabinet), a [national unity government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_unity_government) to which FdI was the only opposition party. Following the [fall of the Draghi government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fall_of_the_Draghi_government), FdI won the [2022 general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Italian_general_election).

Meloni is a [conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservatism_in_Italy) and [right-wing populist](8354382.md) who is also a critic of [globalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globalism). She is opposed to [euthanasia](9587.md), [same-sex marriage](92656.md), and [same-sex parenting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_parenting), stating that [nuclear families](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_families) are exclusively headed by male–female pairs. Meloni supported (but never enacted) a naval blockade to halt [illegal immigration](7124412.md), and she has been described as [xenophobic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xenophobic) and [Islamophobic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamophobic) by some critics. A supporter of [NATO](21133.md), she maintains [Eurosceptic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurosceptic) views regarding the [European Union](9317.md) (EU), views she describes as "Eurorealist". She favoured improved [relations with Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italy%E2%80%93Russia_relations) before the [2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine](79400268.md), which she condemned, pledging to keep sending arms to Ukraine. In 2024, *[Forbes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes)* ranked Meloni as the [third-most-powerful woman in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes_list_of_the_World's_100_Most_Powerful_Women) and she was listed amongst the [most influential people in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_100) by *[Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_\(magazine\))* magazine, while *[Politico](9172097.md)* ranked her as the most powerful person in Europe in 2025.

## Early life

Giorgia Meloni was born on 15 January 1977 in [Rome](25458.md), Italy.[^cite_note-Pietromarchi_2022-3][^cite_note-Biography-4] Her father, Francesco Meloni, was the son of Nino Meloni, a radio director from [Sardinia](29376.md), and the actress [Zoe Incrocci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zoe_Incrocci) from [Lombardy](43807.md).[^cite_note-5] Meloni's mother, Anna Paratore, is from [Sicily](27619.md). Her father was a [tax advisor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tax_advisor) and, according to some political profiles, had [communist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist) sympathies and voted for the [Italian Communist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Communist_Party), while her mother later became a novelist.[^cite_note-corriere.it-6][^cite_note-7][^cite_note-trtworld.com-8]

Her father abandoned the family in 1978 when she was one year old, moving to the [Canary Islands](5717.md) and remarrying. Meloni has four half-siblings from her father's second marriage.[^cite_note-Dell'Arti_&_Spada_2014-9] Seventeen years later, in 1995, he was convicted of drug trafficking and sentenced to nine years in a Spanish prison. He last contacted Meloni in 2006, when she became the vice-president of the Chamber of Deputies.[^cite_note-10][^cite_note-11] Legal documents have revealed an alleged indirect economic link through a network of real estate companies in which the ex-wife Anna Paratore, mother of Giorgia Meloni, was a partner at various times.[^cite_note-12][^cite_note-13]

Meloni was raised in the working-class district of [Garbatella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garbatella) in Rome, moving there after the more affluent home she had first lived in as an infant with her parents was destroyed in a [house fire](3326072.md) a few years after her father left. Her upbringing has been described by her family as impoverished.[^cite_note-corriere.it-6] In her autobiography, Meloni wrote that her childhood and her family's breakdown influenced her political outlook.[^cite_note-trtworld.com-8][^cite_note-14][^cite_note-Dell'Arti_&_Spada_2014-9][^cite_note-Brezar_2022-15] Meloni has a sister, [Arianna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arianna_Meloni), who was born in 1975 and has been appointed head of the party's political secretariat in 2023. Arianna was also partnered with [Francesco Lollobrigida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francesco_Lollobrigida),[^cite_note-16] who has been [Minister of Agriculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Minister_of_Agriculture) since 22 October 2022.[^cite_note-17]

### Education and early political activism

In 1992, at 15 years of age, Meloni joined the [Youth Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Youth_Front_\(Italy\)), the youth wing of the [Italian Social Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Social_Movement) (MSI), a neo-fascist political party that dissolved in 1995.[^cite_note-Pietromarchi_2022-3] During this time, she founded the student coordination *Gli Antenati* ('The Ancestors'), which took part in the protest against the public education reform promoted by minister [Rosa Russo Iervolino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosa_Russo_Iervolino).[^cite_note-18] In 1996, she became the national leader of [Student Action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Student_Action_\(Italy\)), the student movement of the post-fascist [National Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Alliance_\(Italy\)) (AN), the [national-conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National-conservative) heir of the MSI, representing this movement in the Student Associations Forum established by the [Italian Ministry of Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Ministry_of_Education).[^cite_note-19]

In 1998, after winning the primary election, Meloni was elected as a councillor of the [province of Rome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Province_of_Rome), holding this position until 2002. She was elected national director in 2000 and became the first woman president of [Youth Action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Youth_Action_\(Italy\)), the AN youth wing, in 2004. During these years, she worked as a nanny, waitress, and bartender at the [Piper Club ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piper_Club?action=edit\&redlink=1), one of the most famous [nightclubs](19321330.md) in Rome.[^cite_note-20][^cite_note-21]

Meloni graduated from Istituto tecnico professionale di Stato Amerigo Vespucci[^cite_note-money.it/che-scuola-22][^cite_note-amerigovespucci.edu.it/la-storia-23] in 1996.[^cite_note-Biography-4][^cite_note-24] After her election to the [Italian Parliament](1417859.md) in 2006, she declared in her [curriculum vitae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curriculum_vitae) that she obtained a high school diploma in languages with the final mark of 60/60, and "Diploma di liceo linguistico; Giornalista".[^cite_note-camera.it/leg-25] This created some controversy, as *Istituto tecnico professionale di Stato Amerigo Vespucci*[^cite_note-money.it/che-scuola-22][^cite_note-amerigovespucci.edu.it/la-storia-23] was not a foreign language high school and was not qualified to issue any diploma in languages; instead, it was a Hospitality Institute (see *[istituto tecnico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Istituto_tecnico))* specialised in issuing professional diplomas for job titles such as chef, waiter, entertainer, tour guide, hostess, depending on the course of studies chosen by the student. It is unknown what course of studies Meloni selected at *Istituto tecnico professionale di Stato Amerigo Vespucci*.[^cite_note-amerigovespucci.edu.it/la-storia-23][^cite_note-money.it/che-scuola-22] Meloni mentioned that the Hospitality Institute she attended became the *Centro di Formazione Professionale Ernesto Nathan* issuing diplomas in foreign languages. However, training centres are not allowed to issue diplomas. The *Centro di Formazione Professionale Ernesto Nathan* issues qualifications for beauticians and hairdressers.[^cite_note-26]

## Political career

### Minister of Youth

2008 portrait of Meloni for the Chamber of Deputies

Meloni as Minister for Youth Policies in 2008

In the [2006 Italian general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Italian_general_election), she was elected to the [Chamber of Deputies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chamber_of_Deputies_\(Italy\)) as a member of the [National Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Alliance_\(Italy\)) (AN), where she became its youngest ever vice-president.[^cite_note-27] In 2006, she also started to work as a journalist.[^cite_note-28] In 2006, Meloni defended the laws passed by the [third Berlusconi government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_Berlusconi_government) that benefited companies of the prime minister and media mogul [Silvio Berlusconi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silvio_Berlusconi) and also delayed ongoing trials involving him. Meloni said: "it is necessary to contextualise them. Those are laws that Silvio Berlusconi made for himself. But they are perfectly fair laws."[^cite_note-29]

After the [general election in April 2008](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Italian_general_election), she, then 31 years old, was appointed [Minister for Youth Policies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_for_Youth_Policies) in the [fourth Berlusconi government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fourth_Berlusconi_government), a position she held until 16 November 2011, when Berlusconi was forced to resign as the prime minister amid a financial crisis and [public protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Rome_demonstration).[^cite_note-30] She was the second youngest-ever minister in the history of [Italy since 1861](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Italy).[^cite_note-Il_Sole_24_Ore_2013-31] In August 2008, she invited Italian athletes to boycott the opening ceremony of the [Beijing Olympic Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Summer_Olympics) in disagreement with [the Chinese policy implemented towards Tibet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinicization_of_Tibet). This statement was criticised by Berlusconi and by foreign affairs minister [Franco Frattini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franco_Frattini).[^cite_note-32]

In 2009, her party merged with [Forza Italia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forza_Italia_\(1994\)) (FI) into [The People of Freedom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_People_of_Freedom) (PdL) and she took over the presidency of the united party's youth section, called [Young Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Young_Italy_\(2009\)).[^cite_note-Il_Sole_24_Ore_2013-31] In the same year, she voted in favour of a decree law against [euthanasia](9587.md).[^cite_note-33]

In November 2010, on behalf of the ministry, she presented a €300 million package (equivalent to €396.77 million in 2023) called the "Right to the Future". It was aimed at investing in young people and contained five initiatives, including incentives for new entrepreneurs, bonuses in favour of temporary workers, and loans for deserving students.[^cite_note-34]

In November 2012, she announced her bid to contest the PdL leadership against [Angelino Alfano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angelino_Alfano), in opposition to the party's support of the [Monti government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monti_government). After the cancellation of the primaries, she teamed up with fellow politicians [Ignazio La Russa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ignazio_La_Russa) and [Guido Crosetto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guido_Crosetto) to set out an anti-Monti policy, asking for renewal within the party and being also critical of the leadership of Berlusconi.[^cite_note-35][^cite_note-36]

### Leader of Brothers of Italy

In December 2012, Meloni, La Russa, and Crosetto founded a new political movement, [Brothers of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brothers_of_Italy) (FdI), whose name comes from the words of the [Italian national anthem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_national_anthem).[^cite_note-37][^cite_note-38][^cite_note-39] In the [2013 Italian general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Italian_general_election), she stood as part of Berlusconi's [centre-right coalition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centre-right_coalition_\(Italy\)) and received 2.0% of the vote and 9 seats.[^cite_note-40] She was re-elected to the Chamber of Deputies for [Lombardy](43807.md) and was later appointed the party's leader in the house, a position that she would hold until 2014, when she resigned to dedicate herself to the party. She was succeeded by [Fabio Rampelli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fabio_Rampelli).[^cite_note-41]



Meloni with [Guido Crosetto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guido_Crosetto) during an FdI rally in 2014

In March 2014, she became president of FdI, and in April, she was nominated for the [2014 European Parliament election in Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_European_Parliament_election_in_Italy) as the leader of the FdI in all five constituencies. FdI party obtained 3.7% of the votes, not exceeding the threshold of 4%, and she did not become a [Member of the European Parliament](258909.md);[^cite_note-42][^cite_note-43] she received 348,700 votes.[^cite_note-44] On 4 November 2015, she founded [Our Land](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Our_Land_\(Italy\)), a [conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative) political committee in support of her campaigns.[^cite_note-45] Our Land was a parallel organisation to FdI,[^cite_note-46] and aimed at enlarging FdI's popular base.[^cite_note-47]

On 30 January 2016, she participated in Family Day, an [anti-LGBT rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-LGBT_rights) demonstration, declaring herself against [LGBT adoption](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_adoption). At the same Family Day, she announced that she was pregnant; her daughter Ginevra was born on 16 September.[^cite_note-48] In the [2016 Rome municipal election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Rome_municipal_election) in June, she ran for mayor with the support of [Us with Salvini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Us_with_Salvini), a political party led by [Matteo Salvini](1767165.md), and in opposition to the candidate supported by Berlusconi's [Forza Italia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forza_Italia_\(2013\)) (FI), [Alfio Marchini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfio_Marchini). In May 2016, she promised to name a street after [Giorgio Almirante](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giorgio_Almirante) if elected, causing controversy among the local Jewish community and the anti-fascist association [ANPI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ANPI) (Associazione Nazionale Partigiani d'Italia).[^cite_note-49] Meloni won 20.6% of the vote, almost twice that of FI's candidate, but she did not qualify for the run-off, while FdI obtained 12.3% of the vote.[^cite_note-50]

Giorgia Meloni with Matteo Salvini and Silvio Berlusconi in 2018

Meloni with [Matteo Salvini](1767165.md) (centre) and [Silvio Berlusconi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silvio_Berlusconi) (right) in 2018

During the campaign for the [2016 Italian constitutional referendum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Italian_constitutional_referendum) on the reform promoted by the [Renzi government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renzi_government), Meloni founded the "No, Thanks" committee and participated in numerous television debates, including one against the then prime minister [Matteo Renzi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matteo_Renzi).[^cite_note-51] As "No" won with almost 60% of the votes on 4 December, Meloni called for [snap elections](462915.md). When Renzi resigned the next day, she withheld confidence from the next government led by [Paolo Gentiloni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paolo_Gentiloni) on 12 December.[^cite_note-52][^cite_note-53] The 2–3 December 2017 congress of FdI in [Trieste](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trieste) saw the re-election of Meloni as president of the party, as well as a renewal of the party logo and the joining of [Daniela Santanchè](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniela_Santanch%C3%A8), a long-time right-wing politician.[^cite_note-54]

As party leader, she decided to form the alliance with the [League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lega_\(political_party\)) (Lega), led by Salvini, launching several political campaigns with him against the centre-left government led by the [Democratic Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Party_\(Italy\)) (PD), placing FdI in [Eurosceptic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurosceptic) and [right-wing populist](8354382.md) positions.[^cite_note-55] In the [2018 Italian general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Italian_general_election), FdI stood as part of the [centre-right coalition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centre-right_coalition_\(Italy\)),[^cite_note-56] with Berlusconi's FI, Salvini's Lega, and [Raffaele Fitto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raffaele_Fitto)'s [Us with Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Us_with_Italy).[^cite_note-57] Meloni's party obtained 4.4% of the vote and more than three times the seats won in 2013.[^cite_note-58] She was elected to the Chamber of Deputies for the [single-member constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Single-member_constituency) of [Latina, Lazio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latina,_Lazio), with 41% of the vote.[^cite_note-59] The centre-right coalition, in which the League emerged as the main political force, won a [plurality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plurality_\(voting\)) of seats in the Chamber of Deputies; as no political group or party won an outright majority, it resulted in a [hung parliament](652214.md).[^cite_note-60]

Giorgia Meloni and Sergio Mattarella in 2019

Meloni with President [Sergio Mattarella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Mattarella) in 2019

On 19 October 2019, she participated in the Italian Pride rally in Rome against the newly formed [second Conte government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Conte_government). In her speech, she criticised the proposal to replace on the [Italian identity cards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_identity_cards) of minors the wording *father* and *mother* with *parent 1* and *parent 2*, concluding with the slogan "I am Giorgia. I'm a woman, I'm a mother, I'm Italian, I'm Christian".[^cite_note-Pietromarchi_2022-3] This slogan was remixed by two Milanese DJs, becoming a disco-trash catchphrase with millions of views, imitations, and memes on social media, even winning a gold disc.[^cite_note-61] By her own admission in her autobiography, the media and viral success of the remixed music video, having lost the original [satirical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satire) intention in favour of the LGBT community with which it had been created, greatly increased her popularity as a politician, who she said was suddenly transformed "from a boring politician into a curious pop phenomenon".[^cite_note-62]

In February 2021, she joined the [Aspen Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aspen_Institute),[^cite_note-63][^cite_note-64] an international think tank headquartered in Washington, D.C.,[^cite_note-65][^cite_note-66] which includes many financiers, businessmen, and politicians, such as [Giulio Tremonti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giulio_Tremonti).[^cite_note-67][^cite_note-68][^cite_note-69] On 19 February 2021, the [University of Siena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Siena) professor Giovanni Gozzini insulted Meloni calling her vulgar names from a radio; both the president [Sergio Mattarella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Mattarella) and the prime minister [Mario Draghi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mario_Draghi) phoned Meloni and stigmatised Gozzini, who was suspended by the board of his university.[^cite_note-70][^cite_note-71]



Meloni at [CPAC 2022](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Conservative_Political_Action_Conference) in Florida

In October 2021, Meloni signed the [Madrid Charter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madrid_Charter),[^cite_note-72] a 2020 document that describes left-wing groups as enemies of [Ibero-America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibero-America) involved in a "criminal project" that are "under the umbrella of the Cuban regime".[^cite_note-73] It was drafted by [Vox](41928488.md), a Spanish [far-right](221220.md) party. She also took part at Vox's party congress,[^cite_note-74] where she said: "Yes to the natural family. No to the [LGBT lobby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_lobby), yes to sexual identity. No to [gender ideology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_ideology). No to Islamist violence, yes to secure borders. No to mass migration, no to big international finance, no to the bureaucrats of Brussels."[^cite_note-Kirby_2022-75][^cite_note-Sergi_2022-76] In February 2022, Meloni spoke at the annual [Conservative Political Action Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative_Political_Action_Conference) in Florida. She told the attending [American conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_conservative) activists and officials they must defend their views against [progressives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progressives).[^cite_note-77]

### 2022 general election

Main article: [2022 Italian general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Italian_general_election)

Heading into the 2022 Italian general election, a snap election that was called after the [2022 Italian government crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Italian_government_crisis),[^cite_note-78][^cite_note-79] it was agreed among the centre-right coalition that the leader of the party receiving the most votes would be put forward as the prime minister candidate.[^cite_note-80] As of July 2022, FdI was the first party in the coalition according to opinion polling,[^cite_note-Broder_2022-81][^cite_note-82] and she was widely expected to become [Prime Minister of Italy](273741.md) if the centre-right coalition obtained an [absolute majority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Absolute_majority) in Parliament, which would be the most right-wing government in the [history of the Italian Republic](1161302.md) according to some academics.[^cite_note-83]

In an attempt to placate fears among those who describe FdI as [neo-fascist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neo-fascist) or far right,[^cite_note-84] including fears within the [European Commission](9974.md) that she could lead Italy towards Hungary under [Viktor Orbán](750582.md),[^cite_note-Segre_12_August-85] Meloni told the foreign press that [Italian fascism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_fascism) is history. As president of the [European Conservatives and Reformists Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Conservatives_and_Reformists_Party) since 2020,[^cite_note-86] she said she shared the experiences and values of the [Conservative Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative_Party_\(UK\)) in the United Kingdom, [Likud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Likud) in Israel, and the [Republican Party](32070.md) in the United States.[^cite_note-87] Critics were sceptical of her claims, citing her speeches on LGBT rights.[^cite_note-88][^cite_note-BBC_2022-89] She campaigned for lower taxes, less European bureaucracy, and a halt to [illegal immigration](7124412.md) through a [naval blockade](293054.md),[^cite_note-90][^cite_note-Nielsen_2022-91] saying she would put [national interests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_interests) first.[^cite_note-92][^cite_note-93][^cite_note-94]



Meloni and the other members of the [centre-right coalition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centre-right_coalition_\(Italy\)) at the [Quirinal Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quirinal_Palace) in October 2022

In a record-low [voter turnout](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voter_turnout) election,[^cite_note-RAI_turnout-95] [exit polls](665947.md) projected that the centre-right coalition would win a majority of seats in the 2022 general election.[^cite_note-Kirby_2022-75][^cite_note-96][^cite_note-Amante_&_Balmer_2022-97] Meloni was projected to be the winner of the election with FdI receiving a plurality of seats,[^cite_note-98] and per agreement with the centre-right coalition, which held that the largest party in the coalition would nominate the next prime minister,[^cite_note-99] she was the frontrunner and would become the country's first female prime minister.[^cite_note-D'Emilio,_Winfield_&_Zampano_2022-100][^cite_note-101] The [PD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Party_\(Italy\)), head of the [centre-left coalition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centre-left_coalition_\(Italy\)), conceded defeat shortly after the exit polls.[^cite_note-102]

Hungary's Orbán, Poland's [Mateusz Morawiecki](48505487.md), United Kingdom's [Liz Truss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liz_Truss), and [Marine Le Pen](572897.md), former leader of [National Rally](67119.md) (RN) in France, congratulated Meloni.[^cite_note-103] European [radical right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radical_right_\(Europe\)) parties and leaders, such as [Alternative for Germany](38708229.md) and Vox, also celebrated Meloni's results.[^cite_note-DW_2022-104] After many years of absence from politics, [Gianfranco Fini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gianfranco_Fini), former leader of the MSI and AN during the early years of Meloni's political career, said he had voted for her party and described her as an anti-fascist,[^cite_note-105][^cite_note-106] despite her rejection of the label, which she considers to be political.[^cite_note-107][^cite_note-108][^cite_note-109]

Observers have debated how right-wing a Meloni-led government would be, and which label and position on the political spectrum would be more accurate or realistic. Many variously described it as Italy's first far-right-led government since [World War II](32927.md),[^cite_note-DW_2022-104][^cite_note-110] and Meloni as the first far-right leader since [Benito Mussolini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benito_Mussolini), and some academics also described it as the most right-wing government since 1945.[^cite_note-111][^cite_note-112] Many questioned its direction, citing Berlusconi's and Salvini's Russian ties,[^cite_note-113] in contrast to Meloni's [Atlanticism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlanticism).[^cite_note-D'Emilio,_Winfield_&_Zampano_2022-100] Others, such as [Sky News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sky_News), while citing Meloni's and her party's neo-fascist roots, disagreed with the far-right label and said: "Giorgia Meloni is not a fascist."[^cite_note-114] [Steve Sedwick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Sedgwick_\(journalist\)) of [CNBC](223441.md) summarised the discussion, saying: "Have we got a centre-right coalition, have we got a right coalition, have we got a far-right coalition, or have we got a fascist coalition? I have seen all four printed, depending on who you read."[^cite_note-115]

## Prime Minister of Italy (2022–present)

Main article: [Meloni government](72067724.md)

### Government formation

Main article: [2022 Italian government formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Italian_government_formation)



Meloni accepting the task of forming a new government

Immediately after the first meeting of the new legislature, tensions began to grow within the centre-right coalition. On 13 October, Berlusconi refused to support [Ignazio La Russa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ignazio_La_Russa), FdI's candidacy to be [president of the Senate of the Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_the_Senate_of_the_Republic_\(Italy\)). He succeeded in being elected by obtaining 116 votes out of 206 in the first round thanks to the support from opposition parties to the centre-right coalition.[^cite_note-116][^cite_note-117][^cite_note-118] Tensions further grew, in particular between Berlusconi and Meloni, whom Berlusconi described as "patronising, overbearing, arrogant ... \[and] offensive" in a series of written notes in the Senate.[^cite_note-119][^cite_note-120] In the following days, the centre-right coalition parties reached an agreement on the formation of the new cabinet.[^cite_note-121]

On 20 October, consultations between President [Sergio Mattarella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Mattarella) and parties officially began. On the following day, delegates from FdI, the League, FI, and [Civics of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civics_of_Italy)–[Us Moderates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Us_Moderates)–[MAIE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Associative_Movement_Italians_Abroad), announced to Mattarella they had reached an agreement to form a [coalition government](6036.md) with Meloni as [Prime Minister](273741.md).[^cite_note-122][^cite_note-123] In the afternoon, Mattarella summoned Meloni to the [Quirinal Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quirinal_Palace), asking her to form a new government.[^cite_note-124] She accepted the task and on the same day announced the composition of [her cabinet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meloni_Cabinet), which was officially sworn in on 22 October.[^cite_note-125][^cite_note-126][^cite_note-127] She is the first woman to hold the office of Prime Minister of Italy.[^cite_note-128][^cite_note-129][^cite_note-130]

On 25 October, Meloni gave her first official speech as prime minister in front of the [Chamber of Deputies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chamber_of_Deputies_\(Italy\)), before the [confidence vote](208101.md) on her cabinet.[^cite_note-131][^cite_note-132] During her speech, she stressed the weight of being the first woman to serve as head of the Italian government.[^cite_note-133] She thanked several Italian women, including [Tina Anselmi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tina_Anselmi), [Samantha Cristoforetti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samantha_Cristoforetti), [Grazia Deledda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grazia_Deledda), [Oriana Fallaci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oriana_Fallaci), [Nilde Iotti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nilde_Iotti), [Rita Levi-Montalcini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rita_Levi-Montalcini), and [Maria Montessori](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maria_Montessori), who she said, "with the boards of their own examples, built the ladder that today allows me to climb and break the heavy [glass ceiling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glass_ceiling) placed over our heads."[^cite_note-134][^cite_note-135] The government won the confidence vote with a comfortable majority in both houses.[^cite_note-136][^cite_note-137]

In September 2026, Meloni's government became Italy's longest-serving cabinet since [World War II](32927.md), marking a notable period of political stability in a country historically characterized by frequent changes in government.[^cite_note-138]

### Domestic policies



Meloni during a press conference in 2022

One of the first measures implemented by the government regarded [COVID-19](63030231.md) and concerned the complete removal of the [COVID-19 vaccination certificate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_vaccination_certificate), known in Italy as the Green Pass; moreover, non-vaccinated doctors were re-integrated into service.[^cite_note-139] By this time, the government's workforce vaccination mandate had been in place for over one year, rendering the edict largely symbolic. On 31 October, the government approved a decree providing for a penalty of up to six years of imprisonment for illegal parties and rallies.[^cite_note-140] Despite being officially presented as a decree against illegal [rave parties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rave_parties), the law was applicable to any illegal gathering that the public authority deemed dangerous, which garnered criticism,[^cite_note-141] including from jurist Vitalba Azzolini.[^cite_note-142]

The decree also caused a lot of protests from opposition parties and civil rights associations, and was also contested by FI.[^cite_note-143][^cite_note-144][^cite_note-145] According to [Amnesty International](18947898.md), the decree "risked undermining the right to peaceful protest".[^cite_note-146] The Meloni government has rejected the accusations and announced that it will accept minor changes to the text in Parliament.[^cite_note-147][^cite_note-148] In the first weeks after taking office, Meloni implemented stricter policies than previous governments regarding the fight against [illegal immigration](7124412.md).[^cite_note-149]

From an economic point of view, Meloni and her government have decided to prevent the increase in energy prices, in continuity with her predecessor [Mario Draghi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mario_Draghi), by lowering prices, giving subsidies to families and businesses and making new drilling decisions in the Italian seas to increase national gas production. The government also decided to increase the cash ceiling from €2,000 to €5,000.[^cite_note-150]



Meloni with President [Sergio Mattarella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Mattarella) in March 2023

On 26 February 2023, a [boat carrying migrants sank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Calabria_migrant_boat_disaster) amidst harsh weather conditions while trying to land on the coast of Steccato di Cutro, near [Crotone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crotone), Calabria. The boat was carrying between 143 and 200 migrants when it sank, of whom at least 86 died, including 12 children, becoming one of the deadliest naval disasters in recent years.[^cite_note-151] Meloni expressed her "deep sorrow for the many human lives torn away by human traffickers", and condemned the "exchange" of migrants' lives for "the 'price' of a ticket paid by them in the false prospect for a safe voyage".[^cite_note-D'Emilio-2023-152] On 1 March 2023, the new leader of the Democratic Party, [Elly Schlein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elly_Schlein), as well as [More Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/More_Europe) and [Greens and Left Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greens_and_Left_Alliance) asked for the resignation of interior minister [Matteo Piantedosi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matteo_Piantedosi).[^cite_note-153]

In March 2023, Italy's Meloni government approved a draft bill banning the production and commercialisation of [cultured meat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultured_meat) for human and animal consumption;[^cite_note-154][^cite_note-155][^cite_note-156] this move, which the government said was intended to protect food heritage.[^cite_note-157]



Meloni and European Commission president [Ursula von der Leyen](2991932.md) during a visit in the [flooded area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Emilia-Romagna_floods) of Emilia-Romagna

In May 2023, the government had to face [severe floods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Emilia-Romagna_floods) which affected [Emilia-Romagna](162715.md) region,[^cite_note-158] killing 17 people and displacing 50,000 others.[^cite_note-159][^cite_note-160][^cite_note-161] The provisional cost of the damage caused by the floods amounts to more than [€](9472.md)10 billion ([US\$](18717338.md)11 billion).[^cite_note-162][^cite_note-163] On 23 May, Italy's [Council of Ministers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Ministers_\(Italy\)) officially announced the approval of the first law decree in response to the emergency, an estimated €2 billion recovery package that was aimed to public and private businesses, schools, universities, museums and farm workers, among other categories.[^cite_note-164][^cite_note-165][^cite_note-166]

On 25 May, Meloni and Ursula von der Leyen, the president of the European Commission, visited the flooded areas along with Bonaccini. Meloni underlined the strong spirit of the Romagnol people, stating: "Usually, when you lose everything, the prevailing feeling is anger, blame-seeking, or resignation. In Emilia-Romagna I found people shoveling mud with pride in their eyes, saying: all right, we have a problem, but we will solve it, we will rebuild."[^cite_note-167] After weeks of tension within the government and between majority and opposition parties,[^cite_note-Il_Post-2023-168][^cite_note-La_Repubblica-2023-169][^cite_note-Fanpage-2023-170] on 27 June 2023 the Meloni cabinet officially appointed army corps general [Francesco Paolo Figliuolo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francesco_Paolo_Figliuolo) as Extraordinary Commissioner for the Reconstruction.[^cite_note-Baroncini-2023-171]

Italy became the first country to ban cultured meat in November 2023, when the government approved the bill.[^cite_note-172][^cite_note-173]

On 23 January 2024, the bill about "differentiated autonomy" (which aims to give much more autonomy to [Italian regions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regions_of_Italy), for a maximum of 23 specific subjects) was approved in the Senate,[^cite_note-174] and it was then approved by the Chamber on 19 June.[^cite_note-175] The law was criticised by the opposition and by various deputies and presidents of [southern Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Italy) (both from the majority and the opposition, such as [Roberto Occhiuto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberto_Occhiuto) of Forza Italia, president of Calabria, and [Vincenzo De Luca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vincenzo_De_Luca) of the PD, president of Campania),[^cite_note-176] accusing the government of wanting to abandon the southern regions, favoring those of the [north](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Italy), and on 20 July a collection of signatures began to call an abrogative referendum.[^cite_note-177][^cite_note-178][^cite_note-179]

On 12 August 2024, Meloni characterised as "surrealist" the court case initiated by the European Commission over the Italian government's social policy that favoured Italians over recent non-citizen immigrants.[^cite_note-en1-180] On 16 October 2024, the [Senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senate_of_the_Republic_\(Italy\)) voted 84–58 in favour of extending a ban on surrogacy to couples who go abroad to avail themselves of the procedure.[^cite_note-181]

### Law and order policies

On 15 June 2023, the Council of Ministers approved, on the proposal of Minister [Carlo Nordio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlo_Nordio), a bill on justice,[^cite_note-182] which abolished the crime of abuse of office and redesigned the appeal procedures in the first instance initiated by prosecutors against acquittals, as well as the use and dissemination of wiretapping.[^cite_note-183] The bill was then be approved by the Senate on 13 February 2024,[^cite_note-184] and then by the Chamber of Deputies on 10 July.[^cite_note-185]



Meloni decorating the flag of the [Italian Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Army)

On 18 September 2024, the Chamber of Deputies approved the "Safety Bill" (a modified version of the "safety package" previously approved by the Council of Ministers on 16 November 2023),[^cite_note-186] which covered many subjects: terrorism; scams; protection of law enforcement; banning cannabis (in any form, as well as hemp-containing products); imprisonment (2–7 years) for those who illegally occupy a property; increase in penalties in case of protest (including passive resistance to orders) in prison; the need for a residence permit (for non-EU immigrants) to buy a SIM card; possible imprisonment (1 month if alone, 6 months-2 years if with more people) for those implementing road or railway blockades (even as a protest).[^cite_note-187][^cite_note-188]

Minister of the Interior [Matteo Piantedosi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matteo_Piantedosi) confirmed that the rule about blockades could be applied in some [strike](155134.md) cases.[^cite_note-189] The bill was heavily criticised by the oppositions, which labelled it as "liberticidal": in particular, the "anti-Salis rule" (named after [Ilaria Salis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilaria_Salis), an AVS MEP accused of illegal occupation in 2008; however, there was only one identification while she was in the plant at that moment, as the ALER had never initiated investigations, nor criminal or civil cases, in 16 years)[^cite_note-190] and the "anti-Gandhi rule" were criticised, the first one because it aggravated the situation of people who had no home to stay, the second one because it could also prevent peaceful protests, such as those historically led by Gandhi.[^cite_note-191][^cite_note-192][^cite_note-193]

The government has also proposed, within the security bill, the expansion of the powers of the secret services (which could participate in and/or direct terrorist or subversive groups), and the obligation for universities and public research bodies to collaborate with the secret services ([DIS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dipartimento_delle_Informazioni_per_la_Sicurezza), [AISE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agenzia_Informazioni_e_Sicurezza_Esterna), [AISI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agenzia_Informazioni_e_Sicurezza_Interna)), including communicating private information; both of these proposals have been harshly criticised: the first because in Italy the secret services, during the [Years of Lead](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Years_of_Lead_\(Italy\)) between 1960s and 1980s, were involved in illicit activities and massacres during the [Strategy of Tension](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strategy_of_tension),[^cite_note-194][^cite_note-195][^cite_note-196] and the second because it would represent an authoritarian turn on universities, with the risk of violating the privacy and communicating the political opinions of students, professors and researchers.[^cite_note-197][^cite_note-198][^cite_note-199][^cite_note-200]

On 4 April 2025, the government approved a decree-law that almost entirely follows the previous security bill (apart from some changes), which had been sent back to the Chamber due to the lack of financial coverage and doubts raised by the President of the Republic [Sergio Mattarella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Mattarella), in order to speed up the approval process.

### Constitutional reform



Meloni speaking with the press in 2025

In late December 2022, Meloni announced that [Elisabetta Casellati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elisabetta_Casellati), minister for constitutional reforms, would meet with the opposition parties to officially begin the roadmap towards a constitutional reform to strengthen the powers of the prime minister, even if the coalition's electoral program included only the direct election of the president.[^cite_note-201]

On 3 November 2023, Meloni officially presented the reform which provided the direct election of the prime minister by popular vote, the so-called *premierato*, and a new electoral law which gave 55% of parliamentary seats in both houses to the coalition that arrives first in the general election.[^cite_note-202] Following new legislation in Italy passed by the Meloni government, only a child's biological parent can be named on their birth certificate.[^cite_note-203]

#### 2026 referendum

Main article: [2026 Italian constitutional referendum](82240662.md)

In March 2026, Meloni's government held a constitutional referendum where voters were asked whether they approved a [constitutional law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_law), often called the "[Nordio Reform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nordio_Reform)" (*Riforma Nordio*) after [Carlo Nordio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlo_Nordio) (the [Minister of Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_of_Justice_\(Italy\))), that would amend the Italian Constitution in various aspects, most notably by proposing the constitutional separation of career paths between [judges](44719.md) and public [prosecutors](440936.md), the splitting of the [High Council of the Judiciary](52834029.md) (CSM) into two distinct bodies, and the selection of members by [sortition](19288053.md) rather than traditional election, and the establishment of a High Disciplinary Court to oversee disciplinary proceedings.[^cite_note-204]

The government-initiated constitutional bill was approved by the [Senate of the Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senate_of_the_Republic_\(Italy\)) on 30 October 2025. Since it did not reach the qualified two-thirds majority in each chamber of [Italian Parliament](1417859.md) during the parliamentary approval process, pursuant to Article 138 of the Italian Constitution, the necessary signatures were collected to request a confirmatory constitutional referendum. A [quorum](237014.md) is not required for the referendum to be valid, and the Nordio law submitted to the referendum would be promulgated if confirmed by a majority of valid votes. Meloni's government supported and campaigned in favour of its passing. However, the victory of the "No" side was widely seen as a blow to her government and a boost to opposition parties ahead of the [2027 general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Next_Italian_general_election).[^cite_note-205]

### Foreign policy

See also: [List of international prime ministerial trips made by Giorgia Meloni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_international_prime_ministerial_trips_made_by_Giorgia_Meloni)



Meloni with Indian prime minister [Narendra Modi](444222.md) in March 2023

The first foreign leader met by Meloni was the French president [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md), who was in [Rome](25458.md) on 23 October 2023 to meet President [Sergio Mattarella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Mattarella) and [Pope Francis](1687680.md), and had a bilateral meeting with Meloni, primarily focused on the ongoing energy crisis and the [Russian invasion of Ukraine](79400268.md).[^cite_note-206] On 3 November 2023, Meloni met [European Union](9317.md) (EU) leaders such as [Ursula von der Leyen](2991932.md), [Charles Michel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Michel), [Paolo Gentiloni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paolo_Gentiloni), [Roberta Metsola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberta_Metsola), and other politicians in Brussels.[^cite_note-207]

On 7 November 2023, Meloni took part in her first international summit, the [United Nations COP27](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_United_Nations_Climate_Change_Conference) (Climate Change Conference) in [Sharm El Sheikh](419335.md), Egypt. During her speech, Meloni stated: "Italy remains strongly convinced of its commitment to decarbonisation in compliance with the [Paris Agreement](30242372.md). We must diversify energy suppliers, in close collaboration with African countries."[^cite_note-208] During the conference, the prime minister also had a bilateral meeting with the Egyptian president [Abdel Fattah el-Sisi](36703624.md).[^cite_note-209] In the following week, Meloni participated in the [G20](2206608.md) summit in [Bali](4147.md), Indonesia,[^cite_note-210] where she had her first bilateral meeting with the U.S. president [Joe Biden](145422.md) on 15 November 2023.[^cite_note-211][^cite_note-212]



Meloni with U.S. president [Joe Biden](145422.md) at the [White House](33057.md) in July 2023

In January 2023, Meloni visited [Algeria](358.md), where she met President [Abdelmadjid Tebboune](54133968.md) with whom she signed a deal regarding gas supply to Italy.[^cite_note-213] Thanks to this deal, Algeria will become Italy's largest gas supplier.[^cite_note-214]

On 22 February 2023, Meloni visited [Ukraine](31750.md) and met with President [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md) to discuss the ongoing [Russian invasion](79400268.md). Meloni also visited [Bucha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bucha,_Kyiv_Oblast), in the suburbs of [Kyiv](585629.md), where the Russian forces [killed](70455343.md) more than 400 Ukrainians in March 2022.[^cite_note-215] Meloni stressed that Ukraine can count on Italy, adding, "we have been with Ukraine from the beginning and will be until the end."[^cite_note-216] She was an ally of Polish prime minister [Mateusz Morawiecki](48505487.md) and has praised [Poland](22936.md)'s support for Ukraine and Poland's acceptance of large numbers of [Ukrainian refugees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_refugee_crisis_\(2022%E2%80%93present\)).[^cite_note-217]

On 2 March 2023, Meloni visited [India](14533.md), where she met [Prime Minister](24452.md) [Narendra Modi](444222.md) and President [Droupadi Murmu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Droupadi_Murmu).[^cite_note-218] During a press conference, Meloni praised Modi and his policies, describing him as the "most loved leader in the world".[^cite_note-219] In March 2023, she hosted [Israeli](9282173.md) Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) in Rome.[^cite_note-220][^cite_note-221]



Meloni with Egyptian president [Abdel Fattah el-Sisi](36703624.md) and European Commission president [Ursula von der Leyen](2991932.md) in Cairo, 17 March 2024

In April 2023, Meloni had an official visit to [Ethiopia](187749.md), where she met Prime Minister [Abiy Ahmed](56624029.md) and Somali president [Hassan Sheikh Mohamud](36973989.md). In [Addis Ababa](52593.md), Meloni announced the so-called "[Mattei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enrico_Mattei) Plan" by the Italian government regarding investments in the African continent.[^cite_note-222][^cite_note-223] Meloni was the first Western leader to visit Ethiopia since the end of the [Tigray War](65760352.md).[^cite_note-224] During the visit, she also had a bilateral meeting with the chair of the [African Union Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_Union_Commission) [Moussa Faki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moussa_Faki).[^cite_note-225]

In May 2023, Meloni attended the [49th G7 summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/49th_G7_summit) in [Hiroshima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hiroshima), Japan. On 16 July, Prime Minister Meloni, along with European Commission president Ursula von der Leyen and Dutch Prime Minister [Mark Rutte](4321056.md), travelled to [Tunis](57659.md) in order to sign an agreement with President [Kais Saied](61361599.md) regarding the strengthening of the economic partnership between Europe and Tunisia, the European diplomatic support for the disbursement of the loan from [IMF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IMF) and, especially, the fight against irregular migration flows.[^cite_note-226] She considered withdrawing from China's [Belt and Road Initiative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belt_and_Road_Initiative).[^cite_note-227]



Meloni and the other [G7](1196634.md) leaders during the [50th summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/50th_G7_summit) in Italy, June 2024

In July 2023, she had an official trip to the United States. On 27 July, Meloni visited the [U.S. Capitol](31979.md) where she met with [Speaker of the House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speaker_of_the_United_States_House_of_Representatives) [Kevin McCarthy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_McCarthy), Senate Majority Leader [Chuck Schumer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chuck_Schumer) and Minority Leader [Mitch McConnell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitch_McConnell). Later she met with U.S. president [Joe Biden](145422.md) at the [White House](33057.md), where they discussed many issues, including [Ukraine](31750.md), [China](5405.md), and [Africa](5334607.md). They also talked about the strengthening of economic exchange between the two countries, trade relations between Europe and the U.S., security policies and the forthcoming G7 Italian presidency.[^cite_note-228] After the start of the [Gaza war](74998519.md), Meloni stated her support for "Israel's full right to defend itself in accordance with international law, and to live in peace".[^cite_note-229] In October 2024, she condemned Israeli attacks on [UNIFIL](787766.md) bases in [Lebanon](17771.md).[^cite_note-230]



Meloni and Saudi crown prince [Mohammed bin Salman](38689358.md) at the EU-Gulf Cooperation Council summit in Brussels, 16 October 2024

On 28 April 2024, Meloni announced that she would run for a seat in the [European Parliament](9581.md) in elections due to be held in [June](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_European_Parliament_election).[^cite_note-231] In the election, her party remained the most voted in the country with 28.8% of votes, and Meloni became the most voted candidate in the election.

From 13 to 15 June, Meloni hosted the [50th G7 summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/50th_G7_summit) in [Borgo Egnazia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borgo_Egnazia), Apulia. The topics discussed included the ongoing wars in Ukraine and the [Middle East](74998519.md), [Climate Change](5042951.md), [China](5405.md), migration, and the economy.[^cite_note-232][^cite_note-233] [Francis](1687680.md) became the first [pope](23056.md) to address a G7 summit.[^cite_note-234]

On 18 October 2024, Meloni travelled to [Lebanon](17771.md) and assured the solidarity of Italy with Lebanon and [UNIFIL](787766.md) in the [current conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israeli_invasion_of_Lebanon). In a joint statement to the press with Lebanese acting prime minister [Najib Mikati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Najib_Mikati) she concluded: "To conclude – you prime minister you know you can always count on Italy. We will do all that is in our power to return peace and prosperity to this friend nation." (original, she spoke in English)[^cite_note-235]



Meloni with the UK prime minister [Keir Starmer](18681491.md) in 2025

On 19 December 2024, the Italian journalist [Cecilia Sala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cecilia_Sala) was arrested in [Tehran](57654.md) and put in solitary confinement in [Evin Prison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evin_Prison).[^cite_note-236] This became known only on 27 December.[^cite_note-237] On 30 December, Iran's Ministry of Culture finally confirmed that they had arrested her for "violating the laws of the Islamic Republic".[^cite_note-238] Her arrest came three days after Italy arrested Iranian engineer Mohammad Abedini Najafabadi at [Milan Malpensa Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milan_Malpensa_Airport) at the request of the United States, which accused him, together with an accomplice arrested in the country, of conspiring to circumvent embargoes and supplying sophisticated electronic components from the United States to Iran.[^cite_note-239][^cite_note-Raymond-27_Dec_2024-240][^cite_note-Department_of_Justice-16_December_2024-241]

On 3 January 2025, Iran reportedly asked for a [prisoner swap](17209653.md).[^cite_note-242] On 8 January 2025, Meloni announced that Sala had been released by Iranian authorities and had left the country,[^cite_note-243] landing at [Rome Ciampino Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rome_Ciampino_Airport) on Wednesday afternoon, where she was welcomed by Meloni herself.[^cite_note-244] On 12 January, Abedini was released back to Iran.[^cite_note-245]

On 19 January 2025, the Libyan general Osama Elmasry Njeem, better known as Almasri, was arrested after the [Juventus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juventus)–[Milan](36511.md) match near the [Juventus Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juventus_Stadium) in [Turin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turin), and transported to the Vallette prison. Almasri was [indicted](18248714.md) by the [International Criminal Court](14880.md) (ICC) of [The Hague](30269.md) for war crimes. Subsequently, the [court of appeal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Court_of_appeal) of Rome ordered his release on 21 January due to the irregularity of the arrest, which resulted from the lack of approval from the Ministry of Justice.[^cite_note-246]

After his release, the Libyan military officer was expelled and repatriated to Libya aboard a [Falcon 900](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falcon_900) aircraft of the Italian intelligence services. On 27 January, following a report filed with the Rome prosecutor's office by lawyer Luigi Li Gotti, the Tribunal of Ministers was involved to assess whether Prime Minister Meloni, Minister of the Interior [Matteo Piantedosi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matteo_Piantedosi), Minister of Justice [Carlo Nordio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlo_Nordio), and Undersecretary to the Presidency of the Council of Ministers [Alfredo Mantovano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfredo_Mantovano) should be investigated for aiding and abetting and embezzlement.[^cite_note-247]

In a video posted on social media, Meloni said: "Curiously, the \[ICC] did so just when this person was about to enter Italian territory, after he had peacefully stayed in three other European states for twelve days."[^cite_note-248] According to writer and journalist [Roberto Saviano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberto_Saviano), the repatriation affair would have been completely buried without the intervention of some Italian media outlets focused on immigration and the Libyan mafia's control over illegal immigration, who were the first to report the news.[^cite_note-249]



Meloni with U.S. president [Donald Trump](4848272.md) at the [White House](33057.md) in April 2025

On 17 April, after discussing with Ursula von der Leyen strategies on how to approach the meeting,[^cite_note-250] she visited [Washington, D.C.](108956.md) and met with U.S. president [Donald Trump](4848272.md) (who had recently imposed [significant tariffs](78448911.md) on almost all foreign imports); this visit constituted the first step towards trade talks between the EU and the US. Meloni received a warm welcome from Trump, who praised her [conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative) stances and accepted her invitation for a state visit to Italy.[^cite_note-251][^cite_note-252]

On 27 August 2025, Meloni criticised Israel's [military actions in Gaza](74998519.md), specifically condemning the [killing of five journalists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Nasser_Hospital_strikes) in an airstrike on [Nasser Hospital](43293381.md) in [Khan Younis](101330.md) as "unjustifiable". While reiterating support for Israel's right to defense following the 7 October attack, she added that the military reaction had "gone beyond the principle of proportionality" and resulted in excessive [civilian casualties](75130785.md).[^cite_note-253]

In September 2025, Meloni faced large [general strikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Italian_general_strikes_and_protests_for_Gaza) for her policies towards the Gaza war and in response to Israeli military attacks on the [Global Sumud Flotilla](80704925.md). Meloni condemned the violence between demonstrators and police forces.[^cite_note-Milone-2025-254] She referred to the protesters as "hooligans", arguing that clashing with law enforcement and damaging public property would not contribute to supporting the Palestinian cause.[^cite_note-reuters-255][^cite_note-toi-256]

Meloni's government took a pro-Israel position throughout the crisis, aligning with Israel and ruling out any recognition of the [State of Palestine](241405.md), unlike several other European countries that had endorsed such recognition during the 80th session of the United Nations General Assembly. Her stance drew criticism from opposition parties, which accused the government of isolating Italy diplomatically.[^cite_note-Milone-2025-254] Meloni refused to recognise the State of Palestine as long as Hamas remained in power in Gaza.[^cite_note-257]

Meloni condemned the 28 February 2026 [missile strike on a school in Minab](82536519.md), Iran, describing the incident as a 'massacre'. She called for accountability for the tragedy, which occurred during [U.S.-Israeli strikes against Iran](82533582.md).[^cite_note-258]
